orders\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eDFT^P03\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eCharge posting\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eFinancial transaction\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eUsed for charge capture interfaces\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003c/tbody\u003e\u003c/table\u003e\u003ch3\u003eMillennium-Specific HL7 v2 Conventions\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eOracle Health Millennium has specific conventions that differ from other EHR implementations:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eIdentifiers.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium uses multiple patient identifier types in PID-3: MRN (Medical Record Number), FIN (Financial Number/visit number), CMRN (Community MRN for multi-facility organizations), and Millennium Person ID. Your integration must handle the correct identifier type for your use case — MRN for longitudinal patient data, FIN for visit-specific data.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCode sets.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium uses internal code values (Cerner Code Values or CVs) alongside standard terminologies. A lab result may include both a LOINC code and a Cerner internal code value. Your message transformation must be able to resolve Cerner code values to standard terminologies.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eEvent sets.\u003c/strong\u003e Orders in Millennium are organized by event sets (categories like Laboratory, Radiology, Pharmacy). ORM/RDE messages include event set information that helps route and categorize orders correctly.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCustom segments.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium can be configured to include custom Z-segments in HL7 messages for site-specific data elements. If you are integrating with a specific Oracle Health customer site, check their interface specification for Z-segments.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eCOI Configuration\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eCerner Open Interface is configured by the Oracle Health customer\u0026#8217;s technical team (or by Oracle Health support for hosted clients). Key COI configuration points:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eOutbound feeds:\u003c/strong\u003e Which message types are sent, what trigger events are included, which patient populations are in scope\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eInbound processing:\u003c/strong\u003e How incoming messages are validated, processed, and applied to Millennium\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNetwork connectivity:\u003c/strong\u003e TCP/IP endpoints, MLLP ports, VPN or private network requirements\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eMessage filtering:\u003c/strong\u003e Which events, locations, or patient types are included/excluded from the feed\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eYour integration team will need to work with the Oracle Health customer\u0026#8217;s interface analyst to configure COI for your specific requirements.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eMillennium Proprietary APIs\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003eBeyond FHIR and HL7 v2, Millennium exposes proprietary APIs for functionality not yet available through standard interfaces.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eMillennium REST APIs\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eCerner developed proprietary REST APIs predating their FHIR implementation. These APIs provide access to Millennium-specific functionality:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eMPages API:\u003c/strong\u003e For building custom clinical views within PowerChart\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDiscern Explorer:\u003c/strong\u003e For advanced clinical data queries and reporting\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eRevenue Cycle APIs:\u003c/strong\u003e For billing, claims, and financial workflows\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eScheduling APIs:\u003c/strong\u003e For advanced scheduling operations beyond what FHIR Appointment supports\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDocument Management APIs:\u003c/strong\u003e For advanced document operations\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eWhen to Use Proprietary APIs\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eUse proprietary Millennium APIs only when FHIR does not cover your use case. Common scenarios:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eBuilding embedded MPages within PowerChart (clinical workflow integration)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eAdvanced revenue cycle operations not yet in FHIR\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eMillennium-specific administrative functions (user management, location configuration)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eLegacy integrations that predate FHIR availability and have not been migrated\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eMigration Risk Warning\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eProprietary Millennium APIs carry significant migration risk. As Oracle evolves the platform toward Oracle Health Cloud, proprietary APIs may be deprecated, restructured, or replaced. Oracle has not published a detailed deprecation timeline, but the strategic direction is clear: FHIR is the long-term API standard.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://healthtech-development.com/\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003eTaction Software\u003c/a\u003e advises clients to build all new integrations on FHIR R4 and migrate existing proprietary API integrations to FHIR when feasible. The migration cost now is lower than the forced migration cost when Oracle eventually deprecates legacy APIs.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eCCDA Document Exchange\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003eOracle Health Millennium supports C-CDA document generation and consumption for clinical document exchange.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eDocument Types Generated by Millennium\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eContinuity of Care Document (CCD):\u003c/strong\u003e Comprehensive patient clinical summary\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDischarge Summary:\u003c/strong\u003e Inpatient discharge documentation\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eReferral Note:\u003c/strong\u003e Clinical summary for specialist referrals\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eProgress Note:\u003c/strong\u003e Outpatient and inpatient encounter documentation\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eOperative Note:\u003c/strong\u003e Surgical procedure documentation\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eConsultation Note:\u003c/strong\u003e Specialist consultation summary\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eHistory and Physical:\u003c/strong\u003e Admission history and physical examination\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eExchange Methods\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCommonWell Health Alliance.\u003c/strong\u003e Cerner was a founding member of CommonWell, a health information exchange network. Millennium has native integration with CommonWell for document query and retrieval across participating organizations.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCarequality.\u003c/strong\u003e CommonWell connects to Carequality, enabling document exchange with Epic (via Care Everywhere) and other Carequality-participating networks. This provides cross-vendor document exchange without point-to-point integration.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDirect messaging.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium supports Direct secure messaging for sending C-CDA documents to specific recipients.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eFHIR DocumentReference.\u003c/strong\u003e C-CDA documents generated by Millennium can be accessed through the FHIR DocumentReference and Binary resources, providing API-based access to clinical documents.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eFor organizations participating in health information exchanges, the CommonWell/Carequality pathway provides the broadest document exchange network with minimal custom integration work.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eSMART on FHIR and CDS Hooks with Oracle Health\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ch3\u003eSMART on FHIR Launch\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eOracle Health Millennium supports the SMART App Launch Framework, enabling third-party applications to launch within the PowerChart clinical context.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eEHR Launch flow:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003col\u003e\u003cli\u003eClinician opens PowerChart and navigates to a patient chart\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eClinician clicks to launch your SMART app\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eMillennium passes launch context (patient ID, encounter ID, user ID, FHIR server URL)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app authenticates via OAuth 2.0 against Millennium\u0026#8217;s authorization server\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app receives an access token scoped to the patient and user context\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app makes FHIR API calls using the access token\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app displays within PowerChart (iframe or separate window)\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ol\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStandalone Launch flow:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003col\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app launches independently (not from within PowerChart)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app redirects to Millennium\u0026#8217;s authorization endpoint\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eUser authenticates and selects a patient\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app receives access token and proceeds with FHIR API calls\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ol\u003e\u003ch3\u003eCDS Hooks\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eOracle Health supports the CDS Hooks specification for real-time clinical decision support:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSupported hooks:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003epatient-view\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — triggered when clinician opens a patient chart\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eorder-select\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — triggered when clinician selects an order\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eorder-sign\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — triggered when clinician signs orders\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eencounter-start\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — triggered at encounter creation\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eencounter-discharge\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — triggered at discharge\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCDS Hook response types:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eInformation cards\u003c/strong\u003e — display alerts, reminders, or relevant information\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSuggestion cards\u003c/strong\u003e — propose specific actions (add an order, update a diagnosis)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eApp link cards\u003c/strong\u003e — launch a SMART app for deeper interaction\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eCDS Hooks integration with Oracle Health requires registration of your CDS service endpoint with the Millennium instance. Each customer site configures which hooks are active and which services are called.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eIntegration Using Mirth Connect\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://admin.tactionsoft.com/mirth-connect/\"\u003eMirth Connect\u003c/a\u003e is widely used for HL7 v2 integration with Oracle Health Millennium. The channel architecture patterns are similar to other EHR integrations but with Millennium-specific considerations.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eChannel Architecture for Oracle Health\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eADT Inbound Channel:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eSource: TCP Listener (MLLP) receiving ADT messages from COI\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTransformer: Parse PID-3 for correct identifier type (MRN vs FIN vs CMRN). Map PV1 segments including Millennium-specific fields. Resolve Cerner code values to standard terminologies where applicable.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eDestination: Your application API or database\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eResponse: ACK/NAK to COI\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eORU Results Inbound Channel:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eSource: TCP Listener receiving ORU^R01 from COI\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTransformer: Parse OBR for order information. Parse OBX for result values — handle numeric, text, coded, and structured result types. Map Cerner internal result codes to LOINC. Handle multi-OBX results (CBC with multiple components).\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eDestination: Your application\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eResponse: ACK\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eORM/RDE Orders Outbound Channel:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eSource: Your application order data (HTTP or database)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTransformer: Build ORM^O01 or RDE^O11 message. Populate MSH with correct sending/receiving application codes for the target Millennium instance. Include Millennium-specific order identifiers and event set codes.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eDestination: TCP Sender (MLLP) to COI inbound port\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eResponse: Parse ACK/NAK from COI\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eMillennium-Specific Mirth Connect Considerations\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eIdentifier handling.\u003c/strong\u003e Build transformer logic that correctly routes based on identifier type. A channel receiving ADT messages must parse PID-3 repeats to extract the right identifier (MRN for patient matching, FIN for visit context).\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCode value resolution.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium HL7 messages frequently include Cerner code values (numeric codes) rather than standard terminology codes. Your Mirth Connect channels need a lookup mechanism — either a database table mapping Cerner CVs to LOINC/SNOMED/ICD codes, or a filter that only processes messages containing standard codes.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eHigh-volume ADT filtering.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium generates ADT^A08 (update) messages at very high volume — any change to a patient record can trigger an A08. Filter A08 messages to process only the specific changes your application cares about (demographic changes, insurance updates, location transfers) rather than processing every A08.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eMulti-facility environments.\u003c/strong\u003e Large Oracle Health customers often run multi-facility Millennium domains where a single Millennium instance serves multiple hospitals and clinics. COI messages include facility identifiers that your Mirth Connect routing must use to direct data to the correct application context.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eAuthentication and Authorization\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ch3\u003eFHIR API Authentication (OAuth 2.0)\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eOracle Health uses OAuth 2.0 for all FHIR API authentication, conforming to the SMART on FHIR specification:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAuthorization Code Grant (user-facing apps):\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app redirects to Millennium\u0026#8217;s authorization endpoint\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eUser authenticates with Millennium credentials\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour app receives authorization code\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eExchange code for access token and refresh token\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eAccess tokens are short-lived (typically 10–30 minutes)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eUse refresh tokens to obtain new access tokens without re-authentication\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eClient Credentials Grant (system-to-system):\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eYour backend service authenticates directly using client ID and client secret (or JWT assertion)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eNo user interaction required\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eUsed for background data synchronization, bulk operations, and system-level integrations\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eRequires system-level scopes approved during application registration\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSMART Scopes:\u003c/strong\u003e Oracle Health supports SMART scopes for fine-grained access control:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003epatient/*.read\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — read all resources for a specific patient\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003epatient/Observation.read\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — read only Observation resources for a patient\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003euser/*.read\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — read resources accessible to the authenticated user\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003esystem/*.read\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — system-level access (for backend services)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003elaunch\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — support EHR launch context\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cspan style=\"color: #993300;\"\u003e\u003ccode\u003eopenid fhirUser\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/span\u003e — identity scopes for user information\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eHL7 v2 Security\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eSame as other HL7 v2 implementations — MLLP does not include native security:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eVPN tunnel between your integration engine and the Oracle Health network\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTLS-wrapped MLLP for encrypted transport\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eIP whitelisting on COI endpoints\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eNetwork segmentation for integration engine hosting\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eAll PHI in transit must be encrypted per \u003ca href=\"https://admin.tactionsoft.com/hipaa-compliant-app-development-sacramento/\"\u003eHIPAA requirements\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eOracle Cloud Identity\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eFor integrations targeting Oracle Health Cloud Infrastructure (OHCI), Oracle Identity Cloud Service (IDCS) or OCI Identity and Access Management (IAM) may be involved as an additional authentication layer. This is an evolving area — check with the specific Oracle Health customer\u0026#8217;s technical team for current authentication requirements.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eData Mapping and Terminology\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ch3\u003eCerner Code Values\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eMillennium uses an internal code value system for clinical terminologies. Every coded element in Millennium — diagnoses, procedures, medications, lab tests, allergy reactions, order types — has a Cerner Code Value (CV) that maps to standard terminologies.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eExample:\u003c/strong\u003e A lab test might have:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eCerner Code Value: 2700653 (internal identifier)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eLOINC: 2345-7 (Glucose [Mass/volume] in Serum or Plasma)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eDisplay name: \u0026#8220;Glucose, Serum\u0026#8221;\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003eYour integration must resolve Cerner code values to standard terminologies (ICD-10, SNOMED CT, LOINC, RxNorm, CPT) for interoperability with other systems and for regulatory compliance.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eResolution approaches:\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eFHIR CodeableConcept:\u003c/strong\u003e When using FHIR APIs, Millennium returns CodeableConcept elements that include both the Cerner code value and standard terminology codes (when mapped). Use the standard terminology code from the coding array.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eHL7 v2 translation tables:\u003c/strong\u003e For HL7 v2 messages, build translation tables in your integration engine that map Cerner CVs to standard codes. Millennium interface analysts can provide code value-to-standard-code mapping exports.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eTerminology service:\u003c/strong\u003e For complex environments, consider a terminology service (FHIR Terminology Service, or a custom mapping database) that resolves codes at runtime.\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eMillennium-Specific Data Conventions\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDate/time format.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium stores all dates in UTC internally. HL7 v2 messages may include timezone offsets, or may be in the local timezone of the facility. Clarify timezone handling with the interface analyst for each customer site.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eName format.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium supports multiple name types (Legal, Display, Maiden, Alias). HL7 PID-5 may contain multiple repetitions for different name types. Your integration should use the Legal name for patient matching and the Display name for user-facing presentation.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eEncounter model.\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium uses a specific encounter hierarchy: Person → Patient (facility-specific) → Encounter → Visit. Understanding this hierarchy is important for correctly associating clinical data with the right patient context, particularly in multi-facility environments.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eCerner Code Program and Developer Ecosystem\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ch3\u003eCerner Code (code.cerner.com)\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eThe Cerner Code program (transitioning to Oracle Health developer resources) provides:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDeveloper documentation\u003c/strong\u003e for FHIR APIs, HL7 v2 specifications, and proprietary APIs\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSandbox environment\u003c/strong\u003e with synthetic patient data for development and testing\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eApplication registration\u003c/strong\u003e for OAuth client credentials and SMART app configuration\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCommunity forums\u003c/strong\u003e for developer support and knowledge sharing\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eApplication Registration Process\u003c/h3\u003e\u003col\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eRegister as a developer\u003c/strong\u003e on the Oracle Health developer portal\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eCreate an application\u003c/strong\u003e — define your application type (patient-facing, provider-facing, system-to-system), required scopes, and redirect URIs\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eDevelop against sandbox\u003c/strong\u003e — Oracle Health provides a multi-tenant sandbox with synthetic data. The sandbox behaves like a production Millennium instance for API calls.\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSubmit for review\u003c/strong\u003e — once development is complete, submit your application for Oracle Health\u0026#8217;s technical and security review\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eApproval and production access\u003c/strong\u003e — after approval, your application is authorized for production access at specific Oracle Health customer sites\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePer-site activation\u003c/strong\u003e — each customer site must independently approve and configure your application\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ol\u003e\u003ch3\u003eComparison to Epic App Orchard\u003c/h3\u003e\u003ctable\u003e\u003cthead\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003cth\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eFactor\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eOracle Health (Cerner Code)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/th\u003e\u003cth\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eEpic (App Orchard/Showroom)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/th\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003c/thead\u003e\u003ctbody\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eRegistration process\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eDeveloper portal self-service\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eApplication submission and review\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eSandbox availability\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eMulti-tenant sandbox, immediate access\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eSandbox through App Orchard, may require approval\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eReview timeline\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003e4–8 weeks typical\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003e2–4 months typical\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003ePer-site activation\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eRequired\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eRequired\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eMarketplace listing\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eOptional (no formal marketplace equivalent to App Orchard)\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eRecommended (many sites require it)\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003ctr\u003e\u003ctd\u003eDocumentation quality\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eGood, transitioning to Oracle format\u003c/td\u003e\u003ctd\u003eComprehensive\u003c/td\u003e\u003c/tr\u003e\u003c/tbody\u003e\u003c/table\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eOracle Health Cloud Infrastructure\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ch3\u003eWhat Is OHCI\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eOracle Health Cloud Infrastructure is Oracle\u0026#8217;s managed cloud environment for hosting Millennium and future Oracle Health products. Organizations migrating to OHCI have their Millennium instances hosted on Oracle Cloud Infrastructure (OCI) with:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eOracle-managed compute, storage, and networking\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eOracle-managed database services\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eIntegrated Oracle Identity and Access Management\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eOracle API Gateway for API management\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eOracle-grade security and compliance infrastructure\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eImpact on Integrations\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eNetwork connectivity changes.\u003c/strong\u003e Organizations migrating from on-premises Millennium to OHCI will have different network endpoints. HL7 v2 interfaces that previously connected to a local data center will need to connect to OCI via VPN or Oracle FastConnect (private network connection).\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAPI endpoint changes.\u003c/strong\u003e FHIR API base URLs may change when migrating to OHCI. Applications using hardcoded endpoint URLs will break — use configuration-driven endpoint management.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eAuthentication layer changes.\u003c/strong\u003e OHCI may introduce OCI IAM as an additional authentication layer for API access. Existing OAuth flows should continue to work, but additional identity provider configuration may be required.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003ePerformance characteristics.\u003c/strong\u003e OHCI hosting can improve API response times and reliability compared to some on-premises deployments. However, network latency may increase for organizations that previously connected to a local Millennium instance on the same network.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003ePlanning for OHCI Transition\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eIf your Oracle Health customer is planning to migrate to OHCI:\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eCatalog all integration endpoints (HL7 v2 ports, FHIR URLs, any proprietary API endpoints)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eEnsure all endpoint configurations are externalized (not hardcoded)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003ePlan for connectivity testing in the OHCI environment before production cutover\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eBudget for integration reconfiguration and testing ($10,000–$30,000 depending on number of interfaces)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eCoordinate timeline with the customer\u0026#8217;s OHCI migration schedule\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eTesting and Go-Live Process\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ch3\u003eDevelopment and Testing Stages\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStage 1: Sandbox Development (4–8 weeks)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eDevelop against Oracle Health\u0026#8217;s multi-tenant sandbox\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTest FHIR API calls with synthetic patient data\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eBuild and test HL7 v2 message parsing and transformation\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eValidate OAuth 2.0 authentication flows\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTest error handling with malformed and edge-case data\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStage 2: Customer-Specific Testing (3–6 weeks)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eConnect to the customer\u0026#8217;s test Millennium environment\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eValidate data mapping with customer-specific Cerner code values\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTest with customer-specific interface configurations (COI settings, custom Z-segments)\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eVerify integration behavior with customer\u0026#8217;s clinical workflows\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eTest with customer\u0026#8217;s user roles and permission configurations\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStage 3: Security Review (2–4 weeks)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eSubmit security documentation to customer\u0026#8217;s IT security team\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eProvide penetration test results\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eDocument data flow and PHI handling\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eReview access controls and audit logging\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eAddress any security findings\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStage 4: User Acceptance Testing (2–3 weeks)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eClinical end users test the integration in a pre-production environment\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eVerify clinical data accuracy and workflow correctness\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eIdentify and resolve usability issues\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eSign-off from clinical stakeholders\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eStage 5: Production Go-Live (1–2 weeks)\u003c/strong\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\u003cul\u003e\u003cli\u003eFinal data synchronization\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eNetwork cutover to production endpoints\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eIntensive monitoring for first 48–72 hours\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eRapid issue resolution\u003c/li\u003e\u003cli\u003eValidation that all interfaces are processing correctly\u003c/li\u003e\u003c/ul\u003e\u003ch3\u003eCentralized vs Distributed Hosting\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003eOne advantage of Oracle Health\u0026#8217;s centralized hosting model: many Oracle Health customers share centrally managed Millennium infrastructure. This can simplify per-site activation compared to Epic\u0026#8217;s per-site deployment model.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003eHowever, multi-facility Millennium domains mean your integration must correctly handle facility-specific data, routing, and configuration even when connecting to a single Millennium instance. A message for Hospital A within the domain must be processed differently than a message for Clinic B within the same domain.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr /\u003e\u003ch2\u003eCommon Integration Challenges and Solutions\u003c/h2\u003e\u003ch3\u003eChallenge: Cerner Code Value Resolution\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eProblem:\u003c/strong\u003e HL7 v2 messages from Millennium contain Cerner-internal code values instead of standard terminologies. Your downstream system expects ICD-10, LOINC, or SNOMED CT codes.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSolution:\u003c/strong\u003e Build a code value translation layer. Request a Cerner code value export from the customer\u0026#8217;s interface analyst. Load these mappings into your integration engine or a shared terminology service. Implement fallback logic for unmapped codes — log them for manual review rather than dropping the message.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eChallenge: High-Volume ADT A08 Messages\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eProblem:\u003c/strong\u003e Millennium generates A08 (patient update) messages for every change to a patient record. In a busy hospital, this can mean thousands of A08 messages per hour, most of which are irrelevant to your application.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSolution:\u003c/strong\u003e Build intelligent A08 filtering in your integration engine. Compare the incoming A08 against the previously stored patient data and process only when fields your application cares about have changed. Alternatively, configure COI to filter A08 events to only specific trigger reasons (if the customer\u0026#8217;s interface team supports this level of filtering).\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eChallenge: Multi-Facility Identifier Management\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eProblem:\u003c/strong\u003e In multi-facility Millennium domains, a single patient may have different MRNs at different facilities within the same Millennium instance. Using the wrong identifier causes data to associate with the wrong patient context.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSolution:\u003c/strong\u003e Use CMRN (Community MRN) for cross-facility patient identification when available. When CMRN is not available, maintain a facility-to-MRN mapping. Always include facility context in your patient matching logic. Confirm identifier strategy with the customer\u0026#8217;s interface analyst before building.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eChallenge: Oracle Platform Transition Uncertainty\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eProblem:\u003c/strong\u003e Developers are uncertain which APIs and integration methods will be supported long-term as Oracle evolves the platform.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSolution:\u003c/strong\u003e Build on FHIR R4 wherever possible — Oracle has committed to FHIR as a long-term standard. Avoid new development on proprietary Millennium APIs. Architect integrations with abstraction layers so the underlying API can be swapped without rebuilding application logic. Monitor Oracle Health developer communications for roadmap updates.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch3\u003eChallenge: Inconsistent Sandbox vs Production Behavior\u003c/h3\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eProblem:\u003c/strong\u003e Integration works perfectly in the Oracle Health sandbox but encounters issues in a customer\u0026#8217;s production Millennium environment due to configuration differences.\u003c/p\u003e\u003cp\u003e\u003cstrong\u003eSolution:\u003c/strong\u003e Treat sandbox development as proof-of-concept, not final validation. Always plan for customer-specific testing in their environment. Build your integration to be configurable (endpoint URLs, code value mappings, identifier types, message filtering rules) rather than hardcoded to sandbox conventions.\u003c/p\u003e\u003chr development costs\u003c/a\u003e including integration budgeting, see our complete pricing guide.\u003c/p\u003e\u003ch2\u003eNext Steps\u003c/h2\u003e\u003cp\u003eOracle Health integration requires navigating both the current Millennium platform and the evolving Oracle Health ecosystem.
